# INTEGRATION_LEDGER — ReGen Civics module library program

Long-lived, committed, spans rounds. Volatile session state lives in `HANDOFF_NEXT_COORDINATOR.md`.
Program documents: `docs/integration-program/INDEX.md`.

> **THE PROTOCOL: every claim carries the ref it was measured at.** "Lane C is done" is unusable.
> "C1 landed at a1b2c3d, eleven gates green, 794 passed / 19 skipped against a pre-change baseline
> of 19, CI run 12345 green on that SHA" is actionable and falsifiable.

> **Done-vocabulary** (house, from regen-ship-gate): **CODED** = edit in the file, read back.
> **VERIFIED** = behaviour confirmed, all gates green, evidence attached. **DONE** = observed live.
> No evidence means the status stays CODED. "Agent reported success" is not evidence.

## §1 Rules

House (from the swarm-supervisor skill, verbatim):
1. One lane owns a file. Contamination is per-HUNK, not per-file: disjoint hunks are safe.
2. Content attributes a commit. Never timing, never topic.
3. `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> <trunk>` before declaring work missing.
4. A green covers only the steps that actually reached. Read skip counts and durations.
5. Land in queue order (§4). Rebase, never delete another lane's work.

Program (Addendum 1 §3):
6. No lane commits to both repos. A cross-repo item is two items with an ordering constraint.
7. No listing advances past a stage whose exit-gate artifact cell in §3a is blank.
8. Commit by pathspec (`git add <paths>` / `git add -p`), never `git add -A` or `git add .`.

Standing guards (Addendum 1 §5):
9. **Stale-base re-dispatch:** no dispatch and no "not implemented" claim without a grep against
   `origin/main` at a named SHA recorded in §0.
10. **Coordinator is not a lane.** Sanctioned coordinator writes are enumerated in §8-R4. Every
    other write is a rule violation logged in §9.
11. **Evidence-free done:** the done-vocabulary above. Lane reports carry file:line, the literal
    gate output line, and the ref.
12. **Reverting another lane:** per-hunk contamination check, pathspec staging, ask the Owns
    column before planning a change to a contended file.
13. **Vendor conversation outruns its gate:** refuse to schedule build work for a listing whose
    §3a stage-1 and stage-3 cells are empty, and say so to Rye in writing.
14. **Stale gate set:** §5 blocks are SHA-stamped and re-read at every session open. Do not take
    the gate list from anyone's memory, including this ledger's previous author.
15. **Attribution confidence rule** (Addendum 2 fix 3): attribution requires a recorded fact that
    discriminates between outcomes. If two or more outcomes are consistent with the evidence, or
    the newest relevant record is older than the liveness window, state what was observed, decline
    to attribute, route to ReGen Civics. **Never route to a vendor on inference.**

## §3 Resource registry

**Machine hazards, appended the day they are reported by anybody:**

- **`EXIT=$?` after a pipe reads the LAST command's status, not the gate's.** Reported by Lane DOORS 2026-08-30, which hit it itself: a `check-voice` run that printed a violation reported exit 0 because the status came from `tail`. Capture the gate's exit code directly, never through a pipe. Same family as the silent zero.
- **`jq` is NOT installed.** Reported by Lane CAPS 2026-08-30 and then used by the coordinator anyway, costing a twenty-minute silent monitor. Use `gh pr checks <n>` with no `--json`, which prints a readable table.
- **`python3` on Windows resolves to a Store stub** that prints an advert and exits 0 without running anything. Use the `py` launcher, or a script that finds its own interpreter.
- **`git show 'ref:path'` needs `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`**, and its failure piped into `grep -c` returns 0, which reads exactly like absent content.
- **`git grep` matches nothing on a leading slash.** Search `"api/foo"`.
- **Node scripts in `regen-integration` must be `.cjs`**; the package is `"type": "module"`, so a `.js` file using `require` dies.
- **`/tmp` in Git Bash is not `/tmp` to Node**, which resolves it to `C:	mp`. Write scratch files to a relative path instead.

A number can be held **four** ways: a remote ref, a local ref in another worktree, an untracked
file on disk, another session's scratchpad worktree. Entries from a handoff are GUESS until
confirmed from disk.

## §3c Contracting entity block

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Contracting party | Church of the Regenerative Earth (CORE), 508(c)(1)(A) |
| EIN | UNRESOLVED |
| Jurisdiction / registered address | UNRESOLVED |
| Signatory + authority | UNRESOLVED (Rye, under CORE's coordination function — needs confirming in writing) |
| Bank account | exists (per charter) |

Open counsel questions and what each blocks:
1. CORE's standing to contract as vendor counterparty (entity docs, signatory authority) — **blocks CORE signing anything** (Saberra stage 3/5 signatures).
2. DPA counterparty structure (CORE as controller/processor posture) — **blocks CORE signing the DPA** (stage 3 exit).
3. Managed billing: resale vs agency — **blocks the invoice template**, default agency until counsel says otherwise.
4. **UBIT**: margin on resold third-party software modules sold to villages that are not church members — 508(c)(1)(A) is still 501(c)(3)-described and still UBIT-subject; answer may be "yes, report on 990-T," may mean a different entity — **blocks the FIRST INVOICE only. Building and piloting proceed; billing does not.** Do not guess; do not let a lane guess.
   4b (added 2026-08-15, R20): the $ReGen builders' pool pays individual builders in a token valued at $0.10 — payouts over $600/yr per person are 1099-reportable (NEC or MISC?); and does a grant-shaped payout to non-members touch the same UBIT analysis or is it plainly an expense? Same accountant, one more line.
   4a (added 2026-08-15, R13): the developer marketplace adds one line to the same ask — if CORE ever takes a margin or listing fee on third-party DEVELOPER modules (not just SaaS vendors), same analysis? V1 avoids it entirely (developers bill forks directly, Connected economics); this gates only a future platform-billed rail.

## §5 Gate sets — verbatim, SHA-stamped. Re-read at session open.

**game-amora** — RE-READ 2026-08-29 from `.github/workflows/ci.yml` at **`b5bed01`**. The version
this file carried until today was stamped at `28dace2` and was **three gates and one budget out of
date**. `node scripts/module-facts.mjs` prints this list straight from `ci.yml` and **is the authority
over this table.** Every lane enumerates the workflow's `run:` steps itself; this set grew twice in
round 5, once while lanes were running.

```
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
pnpm check
npx tsc -p tsconfig.tests.json --noEmit          # run COLD: rm -f node_modules/typescript/tsbuildinfo
node scripts/check-brand-refs.mjs
node scripts/check-voice.mjs
node scripts/check-hyphen-dash.mjs                # NOT in the 28dace2 list
node scripts/check-auth-fetch.mjs
node scripts/check-admin-reach.mjs                # NOT in the 28dace2 list
node scripts/check-save-honesty.mjs               # NEW at b5bed01 (PR #90)
node scripts/check-repo-payloads.mjs              # round 5
node scripts/check-mirror-annotations.mjs         # round 5
node scripts/check-upload-strip.mjs               # round 5
node scripts/check-artifact-budget.mjs
node scripts/check-doc-links.mjs
node scripts/check-route-reachability.mjs         # NOT in the 28dace2 list
node scripts/check-map-routes.mjs                 # round 5
node scripts/check-image-budget.mjs
pnpm build
pnpm test
# bundle budget block
pnpm audit --prod --audit-level high
```

**Bundle budget is now `MAX_MAIN_JS_KB=700` and `MAX_TOTAL_DIST_KB=6600`.** This file said 6000; the
6600 figure was raised under R33 and the gate section never caught up. `check-image-budget.mjs` was
listed at `4aa867d` and survives.

Notes that survive unchanged: brand ratchet — read `$?`, never the last line, never
`--update-baseline`. Hollow-green check — `.env` with `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, vitest Duration in
minutes, skip count against a pre-edit baseline. A push is not a green: read the CI run on the SHA
afterwards. **And new at b5bed01: `pnpm build` can return exit 0 while the libuv abort fires, leaving
`dist/index.js` at the previous commit — the only honest check is
`grep -c "$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)" dist/index.js`.**

**hub** — read from `.github/workflows/ci.yml` blob `8b68747` at `55cff89`, 2026-08-14. House
gates per CLAUDE.md + regen-ship-gate:

```
pnpm gate                                  # truncation audit + typecheck
node scripts/check-migration-numbers.mjs   # BLOCKING in CI, omitted by CLAUDE.md (Lane P, 2026-08-15)
node scripts/check-env-example.mjs         # BLOCKING in CI, omitted by CLAUDE.md (Lane P, 2026-08-15)
pnpm test                                  # + pnpm test:integration when server logic changed
pnpm build                                 # anything affecting the bundle
pnpm audit --audit-level moderate          # ADVISORY (`|| true`) — never blocks
```

CORRECTION 2026-08-15 (Lane P read `.github/workflows/` directly): the hub has **no
bundle-budget gate** — that belongs to game-amora only; the coordinator's memory that "bundle
budget and dependency audit both block" was wrong for the hub. Every hub lane enumerates the
workflow's `run:` steps itself.

Plus `/ship` (GOLDEN_RULE) before any push to main; push to main auto-deploys via Railway — poll
`pnpm railway:deploys` to SUCCESS afterwards. Coordinator's own commits on `wt/integration` are
docs-only and do not touch main.

## §7 Changelog

- 2026-08-30 · **DIALS AT `974f978`, PR #107, AND THE NEAR-MISS WAS FOUR TIMES WORSE THAN THE CORRECTION THAT CAUGHT IT.** CAPS measured about eleven integer descriptions that name what 0 or 1 does and warned that a blanket R79 sweep would damage them. **DIALS measured the real number: 47.** Every one a genuine threshold where 0 is a real setting. **So the brief I nearly sent would have rewritten forty-seven correct strings, not eleven**, and the thing that stopped it was a lane checking a coordinator count it had no obligation to check. Two more brief errors corrected in the same report: **there are 149 dials, not the ninety-six I wrote**, and my claim that CAPS had already landed in `shared/gameVariables.ts` **was false at the moment DIALS started** (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` said no; it landed later as #104 and the lane rebased onto it). **A second production finding nobody asked for:** `GET /api/game/mechanics/history` returns exactly one amendment row for the whole village and it is this dial, printed raw as `"1" -> "0"`, **so the public ledger was showing the founder's own complaint verbatim.** Fixed. **The registry swept for a second dial of this shape with five distinct probes: none.** And the migration exists for a reason correctness did not require: the boolean parser already reads `"0"` as false in either deploy order, but **without the rewrite the founder's single tuned dial would have rendered as a select with no option to land on.** **One thing deliberately left:** `docs/GAME_MECHANICS_AUDIT_2026-07-31.md` already named this exact defect on 2026-07-31, a month before the founder screenshotted it, and the lane refused to edit a dated audit because doing so would erase the record that it had been seen. **That is R83 applied by a lane that was never told about R83.**

- 2026-08-30 · **THE VILLAGE'S HARDCODED POOL NUMBER IS NOT A FALLBACK, IT IS THE ONLY SOURCE.** Sharpening the R80 violation with a measurement: `MODULE_POOL_PER_CYCLE = 10000` carries a comment calling itself the fallback for a village that has not heard the hub's figure yet. **DIALS read `/api/platform/info` and it carries no pool figure at all**, so the fallback is every village today, and `/api/modules/pool` serves the 10000 publicly as `totals.pool` **with nothing marking it as a mirror rather than an authority.** The live hub value is 333. Still deliberately unfixed, because R81 may change the shape of the payout it feeds, but the defect is now measured rather than assumed. Two related sweeps by the same lane: `MODULE_LIBRARY_CONTRACT_VERSION` is the same shape and is already pinned to its document by a test; `MANAGED_LISTING_CAP = 2` matches a contract clause with nothing asserting that it does. **And one doc contradiction found, outside the lane's zone:** `docs/FEEDBACK_HUB_CONTRACT.md:12` claims a default `FEEDBACK_HUB_URL` that the code does not have, while `docs/FORK_RUNBOOK.md` says there is no default and that this is deliberate.

- 2026-08-30 · **DOORS BUILT THE ASK, REFUSED FIVE PREMISES, AND WAS HONEST ABOUT WHAT A CARRIED VOTE DOES NOT DO.** PR #106 at `ab4ef2e`, nine files, all client. **The sentence that makes it worth having:** a carried ask records the village's answer and rings everyone on the roll, **and then somebody with admin still has to turn the module on by hand**, because module lifecycle is written by one admin route and `module_settings` is unreachable from any vote. **The copy says that before the button rather than after.** Refusals: `ModuleCard` renders in exactly ONE place, not several as I briefed; the door belongs on the module's own page rather than the shelf tile, **because the card's root is a single link so a button inside it is a button inside an anchor**, and because a shelf where every un-run tile grows a call to action has started counting what the village has not got, which is R55; the `agreement` wizard walk was unbuildable because **`POST /api/governance/agreements` is named in `wizardConfig.ts:380` and does not exist on the server** (proven with a control at 8 hits); `about` left empty rather than set to a kind, since asking for a module is not a rehearsal of writing an agreement. **Six defects found by its own self-audit after every gate was green, six fixed, and three of them were the same shape: a failed read falling through to a confident claim**, so a dropped request rendered as "this account does not open votes". Writing the test for the first found a fourth, an ordering bug where standing was read before the look that made it unnecessary.

- 2026-08-29 · **A LANE RE-RAN ITS OWN GATE OVER THE MERGED TREE OF TWO LANES, AND CAUGHT A NEIGHBOUR'S QUERY ONE STEP FROM A VIOLATION.** Told about the `governanceApi.ts` overlap, G-E did not take my clean-merge claim on trust: it reproduced `git merge-tree` itself (exit 0, tree `92225be`), **materialised the merged file and read the seam** (G-D changes the two lines immediately before `fetchStanding`, G-E inserts immediately after it, and that one unchanged line is the whole gap), and enumerated the new identifiers both lanes add to `server/index.ts` to prove them disjoint. **Then it found something neither of us had looked for: G-D's `firstTimesFor` runs `SELECT MIN(created_at) FROM ballot_objections WHERE user_id = ?`** - a per-member read of the exact table G-E's new gate exists to police, added by another lane, in the same file, in the same round. **It is not a violation and the gate correctly passes it**, because a `MIN(created_at)` for one person is the private milestone the spec's inherited item 11 asks for and it neither counts, ranks nor compares. **What makes this exemplary is that the lane refused to conclude that from reading:** it ran the gate against the merged tree and watched the count go **12 to 13**, which proves G-D's query was SEEN rather than skipped, then turned that `MIN(created_at)` into a `COUNT(*)` on the merged tree and watched the gate go red naming the file. **A gate that passes a neighbouring lane's query is worthless until you have proved it can see it.** This is the second-order version of the silent-zero rule and it should be standard practice whenever a new gate and a sibling's work land in one round.

The file is **valid UTF-8**, but a NUL makes rg treat it as binary, and the two failure modes differ in the way that matters: an explicit search says "binary file matches", **a DIRECTORY search omits the file with NO MESSAGE AT ALL.** So every rg-based audit this program has run was quietly missing one server library, and the clock lane could not see a file it was auditing. Escaped, and **proven byte-identical at runtime because one site feeds a sha1 that becomes a stored identifier** (strings equal, hashes equal both ways, Map lookup across the two forms returns the same entry). **Same family as the leading-slash grep: the tool succeeds, returns plausible output, and is silently incomplete.**

The mirror lane's first falsification run was **worthless**: `execFileSync` on `vitest.cmd` does not spawn on this Node, so "went red" was a spawn error with empty output. **Read what the red output SAYS, never that it was red.** And CI caught the clock lane's own control test, which branched on the STRING `@@session.time_zone` while CI's MySQL reports `SYSTEM` and runs UTC.
- 2026-08-22 · **A PROCESS CHECK THAT ALWAYS COUNTS ITSELF, and it corrupted the one signal lanes use to resolve contention.** On Windows, `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process | Where-Object CommandLine -like '*vitest*'` **always returns at least the PowerShell process running the query**, because the search string sits in its own command line. **That is exactly the check a lane runs before taking `.test-lock`**, so a count that never drops below one reads as "a sibling is still running" and lanes wait on processes that exited long ago. One lane's sibling counts were inflated by exactly one all evening and it misdiagnosed twice, then caught and corrected itself. The honest form filters on `Name = 'node.exe'` and inspects the command lines afterwards, or excludes `$PID`. **Same family as the leading-slash grep and the Windows main-module guard: the command succeeds, returns plausible output, and is wrong in one environment only.** Added to memory.

- 2026-08-22 · **THE DIST BUDGET IS RECLAIMED, main `b6761fc`** (PR #51). **1104 KB freed without cutting product and without moving the ratchet**, which turned a founder decision into a non-decision. Two findings: (1) every lucide glyph is its own module, so any glyph used by two lazy routes became its own chunk - **80 files of 130-790 bytes spending 278 KB of the ceiling to carry 37 KB of code**; grouping them also dropped MAIN JS 503 to 477 KB because the shell's icons left with them. (2) **32 woff fallback files, 758 KB, that no visitor could ever fetch**: woff2 shipped in every engine BEFORE ES modules did (Chrome 36 vs 61, Firefox 39 vs 60, Safari 10 vs 11, Edge 14 vs 16) and the app boots from a single `<script type="module">`, so a browser old enough to want the fallback cannot run the app at all. Net: block-charged 6536 to 5432 KB, files 280 to 178, headroom 64 KB to 1168 KB. **The durable insight, now documented: the two budgets pull in OPPOSITE directions - MAX_MAIN_JS_KB counts real bytes and rewards SPLITTING a route out, MAX_TOTAL_DIST_KB counts disk blocks and rewards MERGING small chunks. The build was tuned for the first and quietly overspending the second.** Governance UI (#46) rebased onto it at `92478a4` and its 120 KB now fits with a megabyte to spare.

- 2026-08-22 · **THE MAP'S TOUCH NAVIGATION IS FIXED, main `ec8d147`** (PR #48). Measured before and after on one harness with trusted CDP touch at 390x844 hasTouch, n=5 paired alternating reps, every rep identical to 2 decimal places: a 100px one-finger drag moved **200.00 -> 100.00** world px (gain 2.00x -> 1.00x, and after release 256-268 -> 100-107); a CENTRED pinch, which should translate nothing, drifted **+133.12 -> 0.00**, and once momentum landed **+1081 to +1246 -> 0.00**; an off-centre pinch anchor slid **62.48 -> 0.00** screen px; the zoom ceiling reconciled 2.60 -> 3.20 (clampCam is the authority travelTo already obeys). Root cause was the two competing drag implementations, exactly as the research measured; `touch-action:none` is in the patch because it fixes the OTHER half (the browser claiming the pinch) and on its own moved no number. 14 guarded edits in one patch script, idempotency proven on the committed tree (0 applied / 14 skipped / +0 bytes, md5 unchanged), no line carrying escq/escj/escja touched. **The lying gate is fixed too:** verify_features' touch section now uses trusted input and asserts x and y as well as z, and it FAILS on the old behaviour (117 pass / 14 fail against main's artifact vs 128/3 against the new), naming the real numbers. `#ghint` is the first READ of WGATE in the file's history - the hint copy was written long ago and never displayed.
- **Still unsettled and it needs Rye's phone:** whether an iOS Safari child frame can refuse a page-level pinch. Chromium cannot answer it and the literature does not. ## §8 Coordinator rulings

- **R89** (2026-08-30, and it is the largest restatement of scope in this program): Rye, on what the platform is for: [**what we're now building is an internal economy running so that doing your governance in Hypha is an "upgrade to more powerful tools" not mandatory to run the whole custom game to be governed by the players and issue all tokens, powers, etc in the Game directly ... To repeat that role is to be a financial and governance engine for these villages and games so it should be able to make all the decisions and mint tokens eternally, govern the platform directly (with no admins, using their own voice rules) and be able to make all of our adjustments and changes so that people don't even need the Hypha platform and they can still do all the accounting and governance operations they need to do - version 1.0 of all those tools in capabilities should exist directly on our platform.**] **Seven checkable claims come out of it:** (1) **no admins** in a launched village, which finishes the R54/R81/R85 arc; (2) **mint all tokens internally and eternally, by governance**; (3) **issue all POWERS in the Game directly** too, since a capability is value the same way a token is; (4) **all accounting operations without Hypha**; (5) **all governance operations without Hypha, version 1.0 of every tool**; (6) **HYPHA IS A MODULE AND AN UPGRADE**, for villages that want tokens on Base and stronger governance, never a requirement; (7) **the bridge**, see R88. **This inverts the standing assumption of the whole program.** Every round so far has treated the village product as a game that hands off to Hypha for the serious governance. **It is now the engine, and Hypha is the upgrade.** His own framing of the task: [**go through and really check, red team and look at the foundations of what we've built so far to see if it holds up to this vision**] and [**What's missing from what we've already got today and how could we make this vision of reality while adding the least amount of new features to what we've already built?**] **"Least new features" is a constraint with teeth**: a plan that closes six gaps with one new ballot payload beats six features, and every named gap must come with the nearest thing already built.

- **R88** (2026-08-30, ReGen Civics mints, and one person exists in both places): Rye: [**Regen civics should be able to mint tokens. It's the same ReGen token that it's minting for completed quests. so it should have the ability to mint tokens, and we just wanna have those tokens be able to be sent to the base accounts of people who are regen civics members and their regen-civics account is linked (and we should have it that a regen civics account can be an account in any custom game. and operating in one of their custom games using the same base account and if they are, then they can receive $ReGen from the regen civics codebase and site into their base accounts because they are members of both so we need to design this bridge**] **Three things, and the second is the one nothing is built for.** (1) **The hub mints $ReGen, the same token a completed quest mints.** The R81 audit established that the hub currently creates nothing: the builders pool computes a statement and stops, and value moves when a person reads a CSV and transfers on Hypha. **So this is a genuine new capability rather than a gate on an existing one.** (2) **ONE ACCOUNT ACROSS BOTH PRODUCTS, on the same Base address.** A ReGen Civics account should work as an account in any custom game. **Two products, two databases, two auth systems today.** (3) **A member of both receives hub-minted $ReGen into their Base address.** He names crowdfunding as another surface where the two work together, so **the bridge is a foundation rather than a one-off.**

- **R87** (2026-08-30, what "improve" means, and it settles the question that blocked R84): Rye: [**"Improve a proposal" is that objection cycle you mentioned where those who vote no can list their reasons why and a proposer can go through and integrate objections and resubmit a proposal. This way it's a culture of "creating together" rather than one that "rejects" or "competes" for proposals.**] **So a no is not a verdict, it is an input.** A member voting against says why; the proposer reads the objections, integrates what they can, and resubmits. **The coordinator's recommended shape is adopted with his reasoning attached, and the reasoning is the part that binds a lane:** the mechanism exists to make proposing a shared act rather than a contest. **That rules out designs that are technically equivalent and culturally opposite**, in particular a counter-proposal that competes with the original, which was one of the three options offered. **An `ObjectionPanel` already exists in the client**, so the first question for any lane is what it does today and how short the path is from that to this. **Note what this does NOT change: a ballot still carries or fails.** Improving happens between ballots rather than inside one, which leaves the snapshot law untouched.

- **R86** (2026-08-30, R81 applies to Amora now, and the dry run that has to exist first): Rye: [**IT should apply to Amora instantly but Amora hasnt had the vote to go live yet! So, none of the features should be working. But then we also need a "test the village" option where all the cycles can run rapidly so we can test how they are all working. Lets build this now and then we can (say) but a lunar cycle on a 1 min cycle and we can see how everything runs, which we should do anyway before going live. So the "journey to launch" has this as the second to last button to run a quick test over all settings to see if they would work in production or break in some way.**] **Two parts, and the first is already true.** (1) **MEASURED, not assumed: Amora is not started and nothing is issuing.** Live `GET /api/economy/supply` returns `cycleKey: "lunar-000329"` with `tokens: []`, and that route returns a non-null cycleKey ONLY when `economyReady` passes, so it reached the real supply query and found no issuance rather than short-circuiting on the unready guard. Migration 0112 grandfathers a village only where the ledger already proves a faucet posting, so **Amora produced no row and was never marked started.** Independently corroborated by Lane CAPS re-measuring production at `855075a`: no faucet has ever issued and `gratitude_log` is empty. **No lane is needed to make this true; it is true.** (2) **BUILD THE DRY RUN: a "test the village" mode that compresses the cycle so a founder can watch a full turn of the machinery in minutes rather than a month**, his example being a lunar cycle run at one minute. **It sits on Journey to Launch as the second-to-last button, immediately before launch**, which is the right place for it because the launch vote is irreversible and this is the last chance to find out that a setting breaks. **Note what this quietly requires: every cycle-driven behaviour has to be drivable from something other than the wall clock**, which is a testability property the codebase may or may not already have.

- **R85** (2026-08-30, the founder keeps a key, and there is a second handover): Rye: [**Lets start with the founder having this back-door access until there is a second event where the founder gives away all "training wheels" so to speak. Then the back door is removed, but all named founders have this back door ability until it is taken away.**] **So a village has THREE stages rather than two.** Setup, where the founder builds alone and nothing issues; **launched**, where the village governs and every named founder still holds a back door; and **handover**, a second deliberate event after which the back door is gone. **This resolves the stranding problem I raised against R81 and R74 without weakening either.** A village that cannot assemble 100% quorum is not helpless while the founder still holds a key, and the only permanent trap would be a village that has handed the wheels away AND cannot reach everyone. **It also gives R54 its final shape: admin is scaffolding, and scaffolding now has a written moment where it comes down.** Design consequences a lane must handle: **the back door is held by named founders as a set rather than by one person**; **the handover is an event with a record, not a flag somebody flips**; and **while it exists the back door must be visible**, because R68 already rules that after launch every admin action is seen by all members, and an unseen back door would contradict it.

- **R84** (2026-08-30, answering the reading-A-or-B question and making R68 concrete): Rye, on which reading of "all minting goes through governance" he meant: [**after the game goes live EVERYTHING is a rule the village as a whole needs to set. But a founder type should be able to go through, make a bunch of proposed changes in the admin section (which everyone can access post game going live) then after I have my whole list of proposed changes I would put it up as 1 proposal for the village to ratify, improve, or reject. If approved all changes update automatically.**] **Reading B, and with a mechanism.** The village votes on the RULES; issuance then runs under rules the village already set. **Four things follow, and the third is the one that changes what gets built.** (1) **After launch, EVERYTHING is a village rule**, not merely minting but every setting. (2) **The admin section becomes readable by every member post-launch**, which is R68's "every admin action is available to be SEEN by all members" expressed as a screen rather than as a feed. (3) **The admin section becomes a PROPOSAL COMPOSER: somebody walks through it making changes that are staged rather than applied, then submits the whole list as ONE proposal**, and a carried proposal applies all of them automatically. **That is a batch changeSet with a draft state, and it is a different object from today's one-dial-at-a-time proposal.** (4) **The village can RATIFY, IMPROVE, or REJECT**, and "improve" is a third verb this codebase does not have, because a ballot today carries or fails and cannot be amended. **Somebody has to decide what improving a proposal means before a lane can build it.**

- **R83** (2026-08-30, coordinator ruling, and it fixes a hole a lane correctly refused to fill): **A dated adversarial critique document records what was true when somebody looked, so a fix never edits the finding. It appends one line beneath it: `**Closed <YYYY-MM-DD>, <sha>:** <what shipped, in one sentence>.`** Two refinements Lane CAPS earned by doing it: **bold the label and leave the sentence plain**, so the line matches the `**Issue:**` and `**Fix:**` convention the critique files already use, because a convention that fights its own file gets reverted by the next reader; and **date it to the commit that carries the fix rather than to the day you annotate it**, checking which commit that actually is instead of assuming it is your own tip.** Raised by Lane CAPS, which found that `docs/modules/CRITIQUE-architecture.md:85` still describes the heart-channel concentration bypass as open when **both halves of that finding's own stated Fix had shipped in the lane's own work**, and which **declined to annotate it** on the grounds that the file carries no resolution convention and inventing one mid-resolve, inside another lane's territory, was not its call. **That refusal was right and the convention is the coordinator job.** The reason the annotation is worth the CI cycle it costs: CAPS was correct that `MODULE_DOCS` is an allowlist and the file is not in it, **so no retrieval query can surface it and no member can be misled** - but this program runs audit lanes that read the critique docs directly looking for open defects, and a snapshot claiming a closed defect is open sends a future lane to spend an hour chasing it. **The machine cannot be misled here; a reader can.** Same defect class as an admin page describing a payment that cannot happen, which is the class this program spends most of its time on.

- **R82** (2026-08-30, a batch approval): Rye, on the remaining decision list: [**Go with you other recommendations.**] **So every coordinator recommendation still open is adopted.** Enumerated so nobody has to reconstruct which ones: (1) **SPLIT the two proposal keys** - `mechanics.propose` becomes non-deniable because denying it is total and is denying the say one step earlier, while `proposal.open` stays deniable because the deny leaves drafts and forum intact and a village needs a remedy against ballot flooding, which a per-member-per-cycle dial already provides without naming anybody; (2) **`org.public_people` stays FOUNDER-HELD and the question is closed rather than scheduled**, because it is the one dial where a passing vote cannot be undone for the person it lands on, and the ring is a one-way ceiling; (3) **the seat-history route stays STRICTER than `/api/org`**, with the reason written into the route so the next tidy-up lane does not close it as a defect; (4) **build the "ask the village for this" door** on every module card, opening the existing proposal wizard prefilled, with the module-enabling proposal kind following later; (5) **`/project-history` is called Command Centre in all four places**; (6) **no face recognition** - one reverse-chronological page of every photograph with the flag and request buttons that already exist, the anonymous-request refusal kept, and alt text stripped on takedown; (7) **the `javascript:` call-to-action hole closes with an allowlist** of http, https, mailto and same-origin relative paths. **Item (8), the mint co-signature, is retired by R81 rather than decided.**

- **R81** (2026-08-30, and it is the largest ruling in this program so far): Rye, closing the mint co-signature question by removing its premise: [**This one is a non-issue as we're making changes that any minting of tokens happens after the game is launched and all the power then goes to the village to decide. It requires 3 members to launch a village and pass that proposal and then all minting of tokens go through the governance process. This is huge, ask me any questions on this part and we need to do a full audit across the codebase to address this new reality.**] **So the admin's power to create value unilaterally ends at launch.** Before launch there is no issuance at all (R67); launch is the 100/100 three-member proposal (R74); **after launch, minting is a governance act rather than an administrative one.** Consequences that reach much further than the mint route: (1) **the co-signature question is retired, not answered** - a second steward was a way of restraining a power that is now going away; (2) **`ledger.admin_mint_cycle_cap`, the faucet-scoping question and the approver-equals-recipient question all retire with it**, because they were all ways of bounding a unilateral act; (3) **R54's "admin is scaffolding" now has its sharpest expression: the scaffolding cannot create value**; (4) **it makes the launch proposal load-bearing in a way it was not before** - launch is now the moment the village takes the mint, so a village that cannot reach 100% quorum cannot ever issue, which sharpens the stranding problem GAMESTART reported and refused to build a bypass for. **He has asked for questions and a full codebase audit against this new reality, and both are owed before anything is built.**

- **R79** (2026-08-30, and it generalises past the dial he was looking at): Rye, on a boolean rendered as a number with a paragraph explaining what the number means: [**this first screenshot should really say on/off instead of 1/0 and having to say what they mean! Just say what they do from the start.**] **So a boolean dial renders as ON and OFF, and its description stops spending its first sentences teaching the reader a code.** The general rule underneath, which applies to every dial and not only booleans: **a description exists to say what the setting DOES, never to translate its own storage format into English.** If a reader has to be taught the encoding before they can read the meaning, the control is wrong rather than the copy. This is R73's findability requirement one layer down: **a dial a founder can find and still cannot read is a dial the village does not have.**
- **R80** (2026-08-30, the builders pool, its home and its first number): Rye: [**Make the pool amount 500 but this pool should be governed on the ReGen Civics side and not the Game so it's a setting the ReGen Civics Game mechanics are covering as the "Custom Game Module Creators Pool" tokens we pay out. Then set it to 333 for us to start testing it. These tokens should be distributed (which right now would mean they're all being routed back to the ReGen Civics gratitude pool as extra to that pool since we're the only module creators.**] **Four things.** (1) **The pool is a ReGen Civics setting, not a village one** - it is what the platform pays out across every Game, so it belongs in the hub's Game Mechanics and nowhere else. (2) **It is named "Custom Game Module Creators Pool"**, which says who it is for rather than what it technically is. (3) **Target 500 $ReGen per lunar cycle; set to 333 now as the first test.** **Done: the hub row now reads 333.** (4) **He has stated the expected outcome of the test, which makes it falsifiable:** with only platform-built modules listed, **every one of those 333 should recycle into the ReGen Civics gratitude pool as an addition to it**, and nothing should be owed to anybody. **If the first cycle produces any other result, that is a defect, and the number he named is the check.**

- **R76** (2026-08-29, and it settles the legal question a lane must never guess): Rye: [**Forks should make NO claim to legal entities they should just be [] to be filled out in the admin section on setup along in the "make this yours" admin section**] **So every 508(c)(1)(a), tax, residency and entity claim currently shipped in the code becomes an empty village-configurable field.** Same treatment the land facts got in PR #97: blank by default, filled in by the founder, and **a blank one renders nothing rather than rendering a placeholder.** The reason this is the right shape rather than merely a safe one: a legal claim is the single category where a fork inheriting our text does not merely look wrong, **it makes a statement about itself to a tax authority.** And it belongs in "make this yours" because that is where a village says who it is.
- **R77** (2026-08-29, completing R75): Rye: [**Waning rate is 0%/year default but can be set in the Game Mechanics section.**] **So waning ships built, findable and INERT.** A village that wants it turns it on; every other village never meets it. Three properties this gets right at once: it satisfies R75 without imposing a decay nobody asked for; it is a dial in Game Mechanics so R73's findability requirement covers it; and **a default of zero means the feature can be shipped and read before anyone has to decide the number**, which is the opposite of the usual trap where a default gets chosen by whoever wrote the migration. **Note the unit he specified is per YEAR**, not per cycle, which is a much gentler frame than SEEDS' 15% per day and is right for a village.
- **R78** (2026-08-29, overruling a coordinator concern): Rye: [**You can still show the percentage done in journey to launch**] **So the readiness percentage bar on `/journey-to-launch` stays.** A lane reported it as an R55 violation and the coordinator carried that forward as the clearest case of a shipped surface contradicting a standing ruling. **He has ruled it is not one.** The distinction that makes both true: **R55 forbids rendering a village's HANDOVER as percentage-incomplete** - how much of itself it governs, measured against other villages and against a finished state it can feel behind on. **A setup checklist is a different object**: it is a finite list of things the founder chose to do, it completes, and it is nobody's judgement about the village. **R55 is about not scoring a village's maturity, never about hiding a task list's progress.**

- **R75** (2026-08-29, completing R66's waning half and settling the retention-sweep defect): Rye: [**Members voice should not fully disappear after 1 year it should wane - but waning should only happen if there is voice tokens being used to weight votes. If it's 1 person 1 vote there is no need for this.**] **Three things follow.** (1) **The current behaviour is wrong and is now named as such**: `runRetentionSweep` drops a member below the voting stage at 365 days, which removes them from every future electorate entirely. **Disappearing is not waning.** (2) **Waning is CONDITIONAL on `governance.weight_mode = token`.** In `equal` mode every member weighs 1 and there is nothing to decay; a decay that cannot change a one-person-one-vote outcome is a mechanism with no effect and should not exist. **This is the first ruling that makes a governance behaviour depend on which weight mode a village is in**, and it is correct: the platform's default is `equal`, so for most villages this feature is inert by design. (3) **It reconciles with R65/R66/R71 on the trigger axis**: waning is time-based, applies identically to everyone, and nobody decides it, which is exactly the property that separates legitimate decay from a sanction. **Design note for the lane: SEEDS' own version is the precedent and its arithmetic is a warning.** It depreciates 15% per day over the back half of a cycle and restores in full at the next, which leaves roughly 9% of a member's voice; in a village of twelve, a member away a fortnight returns functionally silent and one member is 8% of the electorate. **A floor, and a rate chosen for a village rather than for a global electorate, are both required.**

- **R74** (2026-08-29, the launch proposal's threshold, completing R67): Rye: [**So the "mark the village launched" button actually generates the first proposal that requires 100% unity and 100% quorum to launch and a minimum of 3 people.**] **So starting the Game is not merely a proposal, it is the only unanimous one.** Every member votes and every member agrees, with a floor of three members. **This is the sharpest possible expression of R68's tiering principle** - thresholds rise with what is being changed, and starting the Game is the most irreversible act a village performs, because it turns on token issuance and converts admin editing into member governance in the same moment. It also sets the top of the scale: with launch at 100/100 and an ordinary quest at the default 20/80, every other proposal kind now has a range to sit inside rather than a single pair of dials. **Note the engineering consequence, which is not a detail: a 100% quorum ballot cannot carry if a single member never votes**, so the launch proposal needs a way to be re-run and it must never be able to strand a village in a state where launch is unreachable. That is a design constraint for the lane, and SEEDS' own precedent is worth reading first - it puts near-unanimity on exactly the two irreversible acts, creating money and ending the system.

- **R72** (2026-08-29, build the payout and make provenance forkable): Rye: [**we need to build the flow for builders to get paid and also build in now the cycling so that the use of regen civics built modules start directing $regen to the gratidue bucket in regen civics and we need to build this all out so that when we fork this code all future games come with this ability to track module use and provenance across the ecosystem.**] **Three builds, and the third is the one that changes the shape of the other two.** (1) **The payout flow itself** - today the trail ends at a CSV and an admin button whose docstring says it moves nothing. (2) **The recycling, now rather than later** - use of ReGen Civics built modules starts directing $ReGen into the ReGen Civics gratitude pool, which is R64 made mechanical. (3) **FORKABLE PROVENANCE: every future Game inherits the ability to track module use and provenance ACROSS THE ECOSYSTEM.** That last clause rules out the current shape, where the hub hand-maintains a builder list and asks one roster village for a manifest: a fork cannot inherit a list it is not on. **Provenance has to travel with the module, and usage has to be reportable by any village to whoever is counting**, which is the R64 "not the governance domain of a single organisation" constraint expressed as an interface rather than as an intention.
- **R73** (2026-08-29, the gratitude caps): Rye: [**Go with your default and make sure these are clearly labeled in the Game Mechanics so founders know what to tweak**] **So the coordinator's recommendation is adopted**: keep `gratitude.base_budget` x stage (100 to 500 in stock config), **retire the flat 30** (`economy.giving_allowance_per_moon`), and **replace both per-recipient caps with one share-of-allowance dial, default 25%**. The reasoning that earned it: the old per-recipient cap of 1 limited the COUNT and never the SIZE, so a member at the top of the ladder could hand one person 500 voting weight in a single send and break no rule; a share is stage-proof and edit-proof, and **a cap of 1/N is the sentence "at least N people" written as one number**. It is also the only lever R66 leaves: **if voice can never be taken away, the rate at which it enters is the whole of the control surface.** **And the second half of the ruling is a requirement, not a nicety: the dials must be clearly labelled in Game Mechanics so a founder knows what to tweak.** A dial a founder cannot find is a dial the village does not have.

- **R71** (2026-08-29, refining R66 and narrowing what R65 removes): Rye: [**Actually I want to clarify that Voice CAN be taken away if a member is removed from a village - this is an admin power initially but turns into a vote by the community later to confirm members who have left - this removes their voice tokens from the pool and should stay at a high unity and quorum rate. Otherwise it gets harder and harder to pass things as members leave and they're still being counted in Quorum.**] **So there are two acts that both reduce somebody's voice, and only one of them is forbidden.** (1) **Stripping voice as a SANCTION while somebody is still a member** - forbidden, R65/R66, and being removed now. (2) **Removing a DEPARTED member's voice from the pool** - legitimate and necessary. **The classifying axis is the TRIGGER, not the effect**: a judgement about how a member behaved versus whether they are still here. That axis is cleaner than the VOICE/EXPRESSION split the coordinator briefed and supersedes it as the primary test. **The failure mode he names is the reason and it is the thing to protect: if a departed member's voice stays in the pool they count toward quorum forever, so every proposal gets harder to pass as the village ages.** A village that lost four of twelve would drift into ungovernability with every remaining member present and willing. **Quorum death by attrition.** Mechanism he specifies: **an admin power initially, becoming a community vote to confirm members who have left**, at a **high unity and quorum** rate. That makes it the second proposal kind after "change the Game" to carry a deliberately raised threshold, and for a related reason: a vote that removes somebody from the roll should need the room to be genuinely present.

- **R64** (2026-08-29, the economic model, and it supersedes the framing of R58c/R59 rather than the arithmetic): Rye: [**for r59 keep it cycling like this (where regen civics built modules pay out regen civics but have it go to the regen civics gratitude pool as the Game tokens $ReGen - it's intentional so that outside module builders are treated the same as regen civics core team acting on equal footing. One day a new organisation could spin up and have created more modules in the Games than groups are using than us and get more of the revenue. The economic model behind regen civics is that of civilizational infrastructure where these tools and currency aren't the governance domain of a single organisation, but very quickly to form a network of them. As the revenue regen civics core team is getting from regen-civics is then distributed to regen civics gratitude system to give out! That way we have a way for these tokens to be distributed to the regen civics community while we use the gratitude system.**] **So the recycling is not a fairness patch, it is the point.** Consequences: (1) ReGen Civics earns on **exactly the same footing** as any outside builder, with no privileged rate and no exemption; (2) what it earns is **not retained** - it flows into the ReGen Civics gratitude pool and is given out to its own community through the gratitude system; (3) **the model is explicitly designed to be LOSABLE** - another organisation building more-used modules should out-earn us, and that is a success condition rather than a risk; (4) **the tools and the currency are not the governance domain of any single organisation**, so nothing may be built that assumes ReGen Civics is permanently the centre. **This retires my own framing of the R59 consequence as "it shrinks what third-party builders receive", which read the ruling as a cost. It is the design.**
- **R65** (2026-08-29): Rye: [**I'd say denying a voice is not a power anyone should hold. Remove this ability for now.**] **So the capability to deny `ballot.vote` is removed outright, not made village-holdable.** This settles the deny-beats-role question that has been open since S36 and overtakes the coordinator's standing recommendation, which was to keep the ordering and make ISSUING a warning a village-held power. **A warning badge may still exist and may still say something true; it may not remove anybody from an electorate.**
- **R66** (2026-08-29, and it generalises R65): Rye: [**This policy shouldn't exist, when voice is earned it should never be force taken away (Though with Hypha rules voice can wane and that can be programmed if they use Hypha who we encourage those who want to do governance professionally to use Hypha).**] **Voice, once earned, is never forcibly removed.** The distinction that survives: **waning is not removal.** A rule under which unused voice decays over time is legitimate and is Hypha's to implement for villages that want professional governance; an act by which one party strips another's earned voice is not. **Any future feature that reduces a member's voice must be time-based and rule-based, never an act somebody performs on somebody else.**
- **R67** (2026-08-29, when a Game becomes real): Rye: [**A game needs 3 people minimum to play (to actually issue tokens) so they can do everything else to set up the game on their own, but once they press "start the Game" this proposal to actually be able to start minting tokens (after the founder sets up all the modules and how the Game is going to run)**] **So a founder may build the whole Game alone, and starting it is a PROPOSAL rather than a button.** Three members minimum, and token issuance does not begin until that proposal carries. **Setup is solitary and starting is collective**, which is the R54 trajectory expressed as a moment rather than a gradient.
- **R68** (2026-08-29, the turning point, and it is the largest governance design decision recorded so far): Rye: [**once the "Game Starts" then members are governing the admin powers. SO there is a turning point where the admin can modify everything while they're designing the Game, but once they click "start the Game" then every admin action is available to be SEEN by all members, and members can make a series of suggested admin changes and put this up as a proposal to be voted on by the members - if it hits the necessary quorum (total percentage of voice tokens being vote) and unity (what percentage says ya vs nay) requirements the rules of the Game evolve. Changing the Game itself should require significantly more % of overall voice to approve a set of changes so people have to become aware of the changes and be wanting them (for them to vote on them) So the governance requirements for changing the Game are different than approving a quest.**] Four things follow: (1) **a before and an after**, with unconstrained admin editing before the start and member governance after it; (2) **every admin action becomes visible to all members** once started, which is a far stronger claim than today's per-act public line; (3) **members compose a SET of suggested admin changes and put the set up as one proposal**; (4) **thresholds are TIERED BY WHAT IS BEING CHANGED** - changing the rules of the Game needs significantly more of the total voice than approving a quest, deliberately, so that a change cannot pass on a quiet week. **This is the first time this program has had a stated reason for different thresholds on different proposal kinds, and the reason is awareness rather than caution.**
- **R69** (2026-08-29): Rye: [**leave it off and retire it. let's just stick with lunar months all around, it's good to be on our own rhythm.**] `gratitude.cycle_mode` is **retired**, not wired. **The lunar cycle is the platform's only rhythm, by decision.** Relayed to Lane HONEST mid-run.
- **R70** (2026-08-29, the audio layer): Rye: [**lets find some open source audio to has as default sets that and new build can add their own.**] **So the manifest ships with real open-source default sets rather than empty.** The standing licence constraint is unchanged and is what makes this non-trivial: **CC0 only**, because a CC-BY sample creates an attribution obligation every fork inherits and silently violates, and the BBC library is out on non-commercial terms. **A fork may add its own sets on top.**

- **R63** (2026-08-29): **ONE unified `swarm-supervisor` skill, and the canonical copy is the project install.** Rye, mid-round: [**I had another session actually create a skill - so make sure your changes are going to that skill here and that we have one unified skill: .claude/skills/swarm-supervisor/ in regen-civics-clean**]. So the project install is canonical; the user-level install at `C:/Users/taren/.claude/skills/swarm-supervisor/` is a synced mirror of it, not a second lineage. **Neither was a superset before the merge**: the project copy carried the frontmatter, the version, §10 close-the-loop, the changelog, the HELD state, the run condition, threading past an uncoordinated session and adversarial verification against named laws; the user-level copy carried §1a fixes-outrank-features, §4a measure-the-baseline, §4b do-not-pay-for-blocked-work and `references/measurement.md`, none of which existed in the project one. **Merged into v1.3.0, 644 lines, four reference files, and the two installs are now byte-identical (`diff -q` clean on all four).** Round 6's lessons went in as folded body rules per §10's own process rather than as an appended list.

- **R62** (2026-08-29): **Standing landing authorization for round 6, and the instruction to run round 7 unattended.** Rye: [**keep going, land them all and take in whatever is in this prompt that would be helpful for you, then surface anything that's on me to know as I didn't ready anything you've produced in this session so far: PROMPT_AMORA_REPO_HYGIENE.md. Then take on round 7 yourself and keep going until everything is done.**] So the coordinator merges each verified PR without a per-lane ask, applies the migrations, verifies live, **and continues into round 7 without waiting for a further instruction.** The hygiene prompt is adopted as round-7 input. **Note what he says about the reporting: he has read none of this session's output**, so the surfacing owes him the whole round rather than the delta since his last message.

- **R61** (2026-08-29, coordinator, Rye may override): **`.test-lock` failed TWICE in one evening under seven concurrent lanes, from two different lanes, so the convention is what broke rather than any one lane's care.** Breach one: a lane's guard printed "LOCK BUSY" and ran the full suite anyway, then an unconditional `rm -f` in its cleanup **deleted a third lane's line**; its own run collided and exited 127. Breach two, 99 seconds after a sibling took the lock: another lane **overwrote that line** and labelled it "run 2", so a third lane's full suite overlapped two siblings against one local MySQL. Both lanes disclosed it themselves, which is the only reason it cost nothing. **The rule for the rest of round 6, sent to every running lane:** (1) READ the lock before taking it and **never overwrite a line that is already there** - if it is held, wait or run targeted suites; (2) say in the report which you did; (3) release only a lock you acquired, **never an unconditional `rm -f`**; (4) **a full suite that overlapped a sibling is not evidence about your code in either direction** - a green from it proves nothing and a red means nothing; (5) filter for siblings on `Name = 'node.exe'`, never on a command line, because on Windows a `CommandLine` filter always matches the process asking. **The structural fix, which this round will not build: the mutex should be something the harness takes rather than something a brief asks for.** A convention that has now failed under load in two consecutive rounds is not a convention, it is a hope.

- **R39** (2026-08-21): **L2's toolcheck boundary exception RATIFIED in-lane.** Finding (L2, verified
  standalone on Node 25): the pinned dialer's custom `lookup` callbacks return a bare string while
  node's net internally asks with `{all:true}` and expects `[{address, family}]`, so EVERY real guarded
  outbound dial dies with "Invalid IP address: undefined". Nobody saw it because every existing consumer
  short-circuits on empty data before dialing (zero tools, module off, zero inbox rows, zero
  subscriptions): a dead guard reading as healthy. L2's fx-rates-daily was the first real dial. Fix: two
  compat edits inside the lookup callbacks honouring `options.all`; pinning semantics unchanged.
  Conditions: own commit with the finding in the body; unit tests for both callback shapes AND the
  unchanged SSRF refusal; one real guarded dial in the fx job's tests; SHA named in the report.
  Inheritors when it lands: L6 agent-inbox delivery, network peer sync, calendar external poll, tools
  link check. Coordinator adds a post-landing live check of the fx job's first tick.
- **R38** (2026-08-21): **Standing landing authorization for round 4** (Rye's words: ["Land the lanes as
  they report and keep going"]). The coordinator merges each lane's PR without a per-lane ask when: the
  lane reported per its brief (tip SHA, gates, skip count), `verify` is SUCCESS on that tip, the diff is
  in-zone, and any security-review findings are fixed or accepted in writing. After each landing: CI on
  the merge SHA, live `/health` marker, live probe, DONE row. Then the queue continues without pause:
  L7 dispatches when L1 lands; L3 when L2 lands; L8's three persona QA passes when every build lane is
  DONE; L4's handover goes whenever the map session wakes. A clause-13/security hit, an out-of-zone
  diff, or a red gate still stops that lane and goes to Rye.
- **R37** (2026-08-21): **Rye widened R36 to every members-only page** (his words: ["Apply the same
  sign-in prompt to all members-only pages"]). Consequence for L1: the shared sign-in gate replaces the
  inline `modules.loaded && !module → NotFound` pattern on EVERY module-gated page (one shared component
  deciding: signed out + id ∈ `signInToSee` → sign-in card with `?next=`; otherwise NotFound as today),
  and the lane also sweeps pages gated by auth alone and reports any that still dead-end a signed-out

  ["Non signed in members should get a prompt to sign-in not a 401 in messaging"]). Measured: anonymous
  `/messages` renders the 404 page because the anonymous manifest omits `members`-lifecycle modules, so

  the anonymous `/api/modules` response gains `signInToSee: [ids]` for modules whose SERVED lifecycle is

  renders the house sign-in card with a `?next=` return when signed out and the id is in `signInToSee`;
  the `modules` array itself is unchanged so anonymous nav behaviour does not move. Messaging is the
  named case; the lane reports (not fixes) sibling pages with the same shape.

The closing verdict for round 2's mobile fixes is therefore PASS on both harm metrics as defined, with the `/quests` 375 CTA and store 360 regression as follow-ups.
- **R25** (2026-08-15): K2's decision RATIFIED — the batch synthesis path ships **off by default** (`assistant.synthesis_batch=false`, founder ring). There was no scheduled synthesis to move; adding one that runs itself would break the automation module's promise that nothing applies itself. The road exists; a village turns it on. Also ratified: the poll job's budget gate is check-only (a timer must never charge the interactive day budget by merely ticking).
- **R24** (2026-08-15): **Assistant cost programme** (Rye: "can we bring that cost down more by
  creating deterministic protocols that give answers and only using a model (and the cheapest
  ones for things that need querying)" → "dispatch lanes to do all that"). Coordinator design,
  grounded in the claude-api reference (cached 2026-06-24) and Lane R's live numbers: (1)
  deterministic answers at zero tokens for structured lookups via a keyword router that selects
  only from `readerCatalog(viewer)` so refusals stay in `callReader` (never a second permission
  system); (2) prefetch the router's readers and answer in ONE POST (halves upstream calls —
  the "server-side pre-step" Saberra's doc 07 already proposed); (3) prompt caching ONLY if the
  natural stable prefix clears Haiku 4.5's **4,096-token** cache minimum — measured with the
  free count-tokens endpoint first, never padded to cross it; (4) the model lever is spent —
  Haiku 4.5 ($1/$5) is the cheapest current model (Haiku 3 retires 2026-04-19; 3.5 retired);
  (5) Batch API at 50% for the non-interactive synthesis path only, admin on-demand synthesis
  stays synchronous. Every path writes a usage row with `path` (deterministic/prefetch/loop/
  batch) so the mix is measurable — a computed-but-never-written metric is the classic tell.
  Migrations: K1 = 0081, K2 = 0082 (0080 reserved for Saberra), both after the four-way scan.
  Expected (to be measured): $0.0081 → ~$0.004–0.005 per tool answer with prefetch; blended
  ~$0.002–0.003 with a deterministic layer catching half; organize's 50 = ~50 questions again.

- **R20** (2026-08-15): **The $ReGen builders' pool** (Rye's design, his words: "ReGen Civics
  pays $ReGen monthly (every lunar cycle) to the most used modules ... similar to the gratitude

  from treasury, on-chain $ReGen on Base — the hub's existing invariant "read-only Base
  queries, no wallet, no signing" HOLDS: the hub COMPUTES a signed distribution statement +
  public page each lunar cycle; a human/treasury EXECUTES the transfer (value moves by a human
  act, as everywhere else); (b) eligible = third-party (`builtBy` ≠ platform), FREE (no
  `pricing`), merged and not withdrawn, at lifecycle ≥ members in a village; paid modules are
  excluded by construction (pricing ⇒ opted out), platform-built and core modules excluded
  (else the pool pays CORE itself); (c) usage metric v1 = number of KNOWN villages running it
  (the hub's roster of real communities × the module ids those villages already publish at
  members+ in `/api/platform/info` — consented, read-only, no new telemetry, gaming-resistant
  because fake forks are not on the roster); v2 activity-weighted via an opt-in relay summary
  (counts, never people); (d) share = plain proportional to usage share of a hub setting
  `pool.regen_per_cycle`, with a dust floor; (e) builder payout identity = optional
  `builtBy.payout {chain:"base", address}` in the registry entry; missing address accrues
  unpaid and the statement says so; (f) counsel line added to §3c: builder payouts over
  $600/yr per person are 1099-reportable — same accountant ask, one more line.

  out the crowdpool bit more - where maybe there's just another page for viewing the crowdpool
  data you'd see on regencivics.earth but talking a more playful and game way following the theme
  of our living map.] and [Let's also talk out the plan for governance audit and what would be the

- **R55** (2026-08-22, the constraint that keeps R54 humane): Rye: [for the majority of villages the core team wants to stay temporary (in fact the shorter the more impressive the feat)! But also recognizing development phases (the child will walk when ready, we don't need to rush but we can still celebrate and work towards these milestones and enjoy the journey)]. **So the handover is a JOURNEY TO CELEBRATE, never a SCORECARD TO FAIL.** A village holding two of twelve powers is not failing, it is young. Design rules that follow: (1) never render the handover as percentage-incomplete, a deficiency, or anything a village could feel behind on; (2) CELEBRATE each power crossing over as an earned milestone using the natural kit's rare-moment primitives, because a power moving to the village is exactly the kind of rare thing celebration is for; (3) frame phases DEVELOPMENTALLY, and the vocabulary already exists in `docs/modules/natural-interface.md` - seed, sprout, sapling, rooted tree, and a moon that is not failing to be full when it is new; (4) NO cross-village ranking or comparison, ever - a leaderboard of self-governance would shame the young villages this platform exists to grow; (5) no nagging, no countdown, no prompt urging a handover before readiness, which is the same "no false urgency" rule R52 put on notifications; (6) a village that hands over FAST may have that honoured as its own story (dates, milestones, what it did), without that becoming a bar others are measured against. **Test: would a two-year-old village and a two-week-old village both feel good opening this surface?**
- **R54** (2026-08-22, and it inverts a flag the coordinator raised): Rye: [These villages are meant to be taken over by the electorate to run the game and put the admins out of a full time job. So we want to promote paths like these]. **The badge audit's finding that "an electorate can vote to enlarge itself" was reported as a governance RISK. It is the DESTINATION.** Admin is scaffolding, not a tier: the intended trajectory is powers draining out of admin and into the village, by vote. Consequences to carry into every governance decision from here: (1) `badge_grant` is not a hazard to constrain, it is the MECHANISM OF HANDOVER and should be made good rather than made safe; (2) every appointment-only capability (`map.edit`, `map.publish`, `feed.announce`, `exchange.manage`, `health.record`, `event.manage`, `org.declare`, `proposal.decide`, `quest.consent`) should be reachable BY VOTE, not only by an admin's grant; (3) the handover should be VISIBLE and worth celebrating - a village should be able to see how much of itself it now governs and what is still admin-held; (4) the guards that matter are the ones protecting the RECORD (the snapshot law, the append-only weight trail, closing as a human act), never guards protecting admin's monopoly. **Design test for anything governance-shaped from here: does this move a power toward the village, or does it entrench the scaffolding?**
- **R52** (2026-08-22, animation): Rye approved the bloom for the `#pnav` collapse ("like a reverse sprouting plant... a sort of blooming flower effect") AND the broader list of where motion earns its place [Love your suggestions on where to add them! Lets do it!]. **The principle, now written into `docs/modules/natural-interface.md` so lanes inherit the judgement: motion that ANSWERS the person is alive, motion that INTERRUPTS them is noise.** Animate state transitions, rare earned moments, arrivals and waiting. Do NOT animate ambient idle motion, anything on every row of a list, anything that moves the camera, or anything a person did not cause. Every effect needs a dignified reduced-motion still state; transform and opacity only; celebration is for RARE things because a product that celebrates everything celebrates nothing. This distinction is the same one the map fix and the Maia camera-consent fix are both built on, which is why it generalises.
- **R51** (2026-08-22): **adding to the running lane is the rule, not the exception.** Rye: [Yes adding to the running lane should be the rule and norm]. Elevated in `~/.claude/skills/swarm-supervisor/SKILL.md` from item 9 of twelve mechanical lessons to one of the two protocols at the head of the coordination section, beside "every claim carries the ref it was measured at". New work touching a surface a lane is already inside goes to that lane as a numbered brief addendum naming why it came to them; never a second lane into the same files, and never queued behind the round. Two narrow exceptions, both about the lane rather than convenience: the lane is finishing and the addendum would delay a landing that matters more, or the work is genuinely a different subsystem that merely shares a directory.
- **R50** (2026-08-22, the living map on mobile): Rye ruled **Option A, full de-iframe** [Great plan lets go option a]. `/map` becomes a direct top-level navigation to the artifact rather than an iframe embed, which REMOVES the unresolved question (an iOS Safari child frame may be unable to refuse a page-level pinch) instead of working around it. Earlier in the same exchange he also withdrew his own "hyper reactive zoom" idea on the evidence [Definitely push back on the hyper reactive zoom - that was just an example, definitely good idea that it's not the right fix!] - the research showed iOS arbitrates on finger count rather than distance, and that the pan is ALREADY running at 2.4x by accident, which is precisely what he was calling "incredibly unresponsive". **Sequencing constraint, recorded so it is not lost:** the de-iframe lane must NOT start until the touch lane (`wt/r5-touch`) lands, because both change `grounds-v0.html` AND `LivingMap.tsx`, and two lanes in that artifact is the most expensive mistake this program has made.
- **R49** (2026-08-22, LAW 5 SETTLED, and the whole findings list approved): Rye ruled [Law 5 - Go with your recommendations - with option 1 expand it to mention each village gets their own internal record but each village decides if they want to formalize it on a blockchain (which one) or use legal documents or both to bridge decisions here. With a link to Hypha with a "we recommend our partner Hypha for recording on Coinbase's Base blockchain").] **Shipped as `bcb0415` on PR #38.** The old law carried "Formal decisions bind on Hypha", which had NO enforcement behind it (its `enforcedBy` only ever enforced the token rule) and stopped being true the day a village could vote here. Split into two laws: "Your record is yours, and you choose how it becomes formal" (record + token rule + the village formalises via blockchain, legal documents, or both) and "The village decides where its decisions bind". `ConstitutionLaw` gained an optional `note` so the Hypha recommendation sits BESIDE a law as advice, never inherited as law by every fork. **The amendment also caught a stale founder-facing string the audit had missed:** `governance.voice_weighting`'s description still told founders "Formal decisions bind on Hypha" - corrected. Case-sensitive grep first confirmed no test asserts either phrase. **Findings list: all 26 items approved** [Everything else is great! Go do good work!] with two amendments - **#5 `hypha.space_id`: BUILD the on-chain provenance for real** [we need to BUILD this on-chain provenance say "it's coming soon" for now and set a reminder to make sure we make this true. This will be a separate build.]; **#6 badges: audit the foundations first** [As your shipping this badge, check the foundations for badges and their ability to grant powers like this to make sure the architecture is what we need it to be to govern abilities like this across the platform.]
- **R47** (2026-08-22, the economy, correcting the coordinator's framing): Rye's words: [Gratitude is just a signal on where to send the pool of tokens founder set to be distributed by Gratitude (so in this case Village Credits can be distributed by how Gratitude flows - gratitude itself is just a signaling tool). But yes we need to add in an ability to book nights, do RSVP's to some events with Village Credits - we can also create a simple send tokens feature that sends our internal tokens around so that users can spend them with each other (think farmers markets at the village and more).] **So the audit's "gratitude buys nothing" finding was half-wrong and the correction matters: gratitude is a ROUTING SIGNAL, never a currency, and its non-spendability is correct by design.** The real defect is narrower and still real: **the pool token it routes (Village Credits) has no sinks.** Ruled build: (1) book a night with Village Credits, (2) RSVP to events with Village Credits, (3) a peer-to-peer send so members spend with each other (village farmers market). **Technical consequence to brief carefully:** every token today is `transferable: false`; P2P send requires flipping that for the CREDIT-kind token only. Recognition-kind (gratitude) stays non-transferable - constitutional, and Rye's framing confirms why. All movement still goes through postTransferPair; only faucet accounts may go negative, so a send with insufficient balance must fail loudly.
