- **R56** (2026-08-22, and it overrides a tone the coordinator briefed): Rye: [Villages can set quorum (or should with the admin screen) to whatever they want so they can set it low or wherever. I saw we start with 80/20 default where 80% unity and 20% quorum. **We dont have to protect them.**] Verified and he is right: `governance.quorum_pct` is min 1 / max 100 / default 20 and `governance.unity_pct` is min 50 / max 100 / default 80 (`shared/gameVariables.ts:485-507`), both editable today in Admin's Game Mechanics tab, which reads every category from `api/admin/variables` and writes `api/admin/variables/:key`. **So a village setting quorum to 1% is exercising a dial, not making a mistake, and no surface may argue with it.** Consequences: (1) an admin screen STATES WHAT IS TRUE and then gets out of the way - a count of members holding no weight is a fact, never a warning, never a block, never a scold; (2) the word "warning" was struck from the doors brief; (3) this generalises past governance to every dial the platform exposes - the platform's job is to make a consequence legible, and the village's job is to choose it; (4) it does NOT weaken the guards that protect THE RECORD (the snapshot law, the append-only weight trail, closing as a human act), which is the R54 distinction restated: guard the record, never the village's freedom to decide. **Test for any cautionary sentence: is this telling them something they cannot see, or telling them what to want?**

- **R57** (2026-08-22, the `/api/org` posture, answering a question that had been open since round 4): Rye: [yes a signed out visitor can see by default but we can have a **"secret society" toggle** for those who want it to be member-only.] **So a village's people, circles and roles are PUBLIC BY DEFAULT, with a village-set lock.** Verified while briefing it, and the ruling turned out to sit on top of a plain bug: `api/org` gates the people tier on `hasCapability("map.viewPeople", ...)` **and all three public pages call it with no Authorization header at all** (`Team.tsx:37`, `Circles.tsx:159`, `Roles.tsx:202` are each a bare `fetch("/api/org")`). **So a SIGNED-IN member holding that capability could not see people either, and the capability has never done anything on those three pages.** The narrow non-negotiable, which sits outside R56 the same way EXIF stripping does: this makes real people's presence publicly visible and they did not vote on it, so the field-by-field list of what crosses into public is a person's-exposure question, not a village dial.
- **R58** (2026-08-22, the Hypha module, four rulings in one message): (a) **THE LISTENER FOLLOWS THE HOSTING RELATIONSHIP** - [our business model is if they're paying us to host we run the Base listener, if they're not then they do]. This retires the single-point-of-failure where "the ReGen hub runs ONE Alchemy listener on Base for every fork"; both paths become first-class and the village is told which one it is on. (b) **DO NOT ARCHITECT AGAINST A FUTURE WRITE** - [eventually we may want to evolve to be able to write directly from our app when that's possible so lets not fight it]. **Read precisely: this does NOT authorise a write today.** Ring 0 still says never mints, moves, or prices, the ledger still enforces it, and the constitution is amended by the founder rather than by a lane. What it rules out is a boundary shaped so a write could never slot in, and a test whose only job is to forbid one forever. (c) **v1.0 COMMERCIALS: every module is FREE to every village, and modules earn $ReGen on usage**; a store where villages pay module authors is a later maybe. So no licence credential and no entitlement gate on this module, and the usage-metering mechanism is a platform-wide question outside any one module's lane. (d) **OPEN TO OTHER DAO STACKS, ONE MODULE EACH** - [each one will be it's own "module" anyway as this is specifically the Hypha module]. So extract the general chain-read and bridge seam, keep this module Hypha-specific, and make a second stack a SIBLING rather than an edit.

- **R59** (2026-08-23, resolving the collision inside R58c): Rye: [**Platform made modules can still earn ReGen to keep the system flowing well but those earnings just cycle back into the bucket to give out again next cycle.**] So the answer to `poolStatus` refusing every platform-built module is neither "exclude them" nor "pay ourselves": **they are ELIGIBLE, they earn, and their share RETURNS TO THE POOL for the next cycle.** Consequences: (1) usage measurement stays honest, because a module the platform wrote is measured on the same footing as one a village wrote; (2) the arithmetic stays whole, because nothing is dropped on the floor; (3) **the recycling must be VISIBLE in whatever surface reports the pool** - an author or a village should be able to see the platform's share going back in rather than into our pocket, and that transparency is the point rather than a nicety; (4) it still requires the metering that does not exist, so the ruling is a shape and not yet a mechanism. Laned as METER with migrations 0101/0102.

- **R60** (2026-08-23, how a swarm session ENDS): Rye: [**swarm sessions should always end in doing 3 unique and complementary perspective QA tests across everything built and touched during that session and closes out only after fixing all the findings of those tests.**] And, in the same breath, on the close-out itself: [**The skill should actually end every session this way - with a clear report of everything shipped, updating the skills with lessons so it's always learning, and creating the new prompt for the next session to pick up more fixes and anything left over.**] **So a round is now closed by four acts, in order: the three QA passes and their fixes; the report of everything shipped in his terms; the skill taught what the round cost; the next session's prompt written.** The QA passes must be **DIFFERENT PERSPECTIVES, never one review at three efforts** - the set adopted is (1) the member's eyes, driving the running product and reading every sentence as a person, which is the pass that catches what code review cannot see; (2) the adversary, attacking the invariants as an ORDINARY MEMBER rather than as an admin, and reporting the survived attacks as well as the successful ones; (3) the operator and the fork, asking of every control whether it saves AND whether anything reads what it saved. They run **READ-ONLY and in parallel** with their own scratch schemas, forbidden from fixing, because three lanes editing one file fight and a QA pass that fixes stops looking; the fix wave follows with disjoint zones. Written into `swarm-supervisor` §7a as step 0.

## §9 Paid lessons (seeded; append the day they happen)

- **`jq` IS NOT INSTALLED ON THIS MACHINE, AND A LANE TOLD ME BEFORE I USED IT ANYWAY.** 2026-08-30, fourth instrument failure of the night and the most avoidable. Lane CAPS said in its own report that `jq` was absent and that it had exercised a workflow step's inputs instead of declaring the step untestable. **Twenty minutes later I armed a CI monitor whose every branch piped `gh pr checks --json` into `jq`.** `gh` returned real JSON, so the not-empty guard passed; `jq` was missing, so every extraction produced an empty string; an empty string is not different from the previous empty string, so nothing ever printed; and the completion test also ran through `jq`, so it never fired either. **The monitor ran its full loop and reported exit 0 with no output**, which is indistinguishable from a run where the checks never settled. The checks had in fact been green the whole time. **Two rules.** (1) **A tool a lane reports missing goes in the hazards list the moment it is reported**, because the next use is usually the coordinator's and usually within the hour. (2) **A poll loop must assert that its EXTRACTOR produced something, not only that its FETCH did.** The fetch succeeding and the parse yielding nothing is the same silent zero this program keeps buying, wearing a new coat. `gh pr checks` without `--json` prints a readable table and needs no parser at all; prefer it.

- **A CHECK THAT PRINTS IS NOT A CHECK. IT HAS TO EXIT NON-ZERO.** 2026-08-30, third instrument failure of the night and the only one that reached a commit message. Amending R83 I ran `node -e '...' && git add ... && git commit`, where the script ended with `console.log("amended:", s.includes(...))`. **It printed `false` and exited 0**, so the `&&` chain sailed on and committed a message claiming a change the file did not contain. The replacement target had not matched, because the real text sat inside backticks and my search string did not. **Two rules out of it.** (1) **A verification inside a chained command must `process.exit(1)` on failure**, or it is decoration; a printed `false` scrolls past and an exit code does not. (2) **When a replacement is the whole point of a script, a zero-match replace is an ERROR, never a no-op.** String replacement fails silently by design: `String.replace` with no match returns the original and reports nothing. **Every edit script since asserts each target was found and names the ones that were not.** Note the shape shared with the borrowed-green an hour earlier: both produced a confident pass from a real measurement that was answering a different question than the one asked.

- **A STATUS CHECK THAT FLATTENS ITS INPUT READS A NEIGHBOUR'S ANSWER.** 2026-08-30, and it is the second time in one night that my own instrument produced a false green. Watching the hub deploy after merging PR #47, I collapsed four lines of `railway:deploys` into one string with `tr '\n' ' '` and then tested it with `case "$s" in *SUCCESS*)`. **The newest deployment was `BUILDING`. A deployment from nine days earlier was `SUCCESS`, in the same flattened string, so the glob matched and the monitor announced the deploy had landed.** The check was not merely wrong, it was wrong in the direction that stops you looking. Same shape as the `wc -l` undercount earlier the same night: **a measurement whose failure mode is a confident pass.** The rule this earns: **when a command returns a LIST and you want a fact about ONE row, extract that row before you test it, never pattern-match the whole output.** Parse the newest line, assert you actually parsed something (an empty parse is an instrument problem and must say so rather than sleeping quietly), and only then read its status. The general form is already in `references/measurement.md` as the silent-zero class; this is its sibling, the **borrowed-green**, where the answer comes from a real row that is not the row you asked about.

- **2026-08-22 · THE MONITOR-YIELD HAS NOW COST FOUR LANES IN ONE ROUND, BECAUSE I FIXED IT IN THE LEDGER AND NOT IN THE BRIEF.** Lanes keep ending a turn with "waiting on the suite" or "the monitors will report", and twice that mid-state was collected as if it were the lane's final answer (once with the fix sitting UNCOMMITTED while the dangerous old tip was still on the remote). I recorded the rule the first time it happened and kept writing briefs that did not contain it. **A lesson in the ledger changes nothing if the briefs do not carry it.** Fixed by adding to the standing house-rules block every brief now opens with: DO NOT END YOUR TURN WHILE YOUR OWN BACKGROUND JOB IS THE ONLY THING HOLDING YOUR RESULT - wait it out inside the turn with a blocking check, or poll it to completion. Same class as the CLAUDE.md staleness this round: a fact written where nobody reads it at the moment of use is not a fact anyone has.

- **2026-08-22 · THE TEST MUTEX IS A CONVENTION NOTHING ENFORCES, AND IT SILENTLY FAILED.** The notify lane's full suite produced a 38-failure cascade, then an unrelated 500 that passed on a quiet re-run. Cause: `wt-r5-ci` was running its own `pnpm test` against the same local MySQL on :3307, and **no `.test-lock` file existed in any worktree** - the lane simply did not take it. Standing clearance says lanes MAY skip the local suite when the mutex is held, which quietly assumes anyone running one takes it. Two consequences: a lane can burn an hour diagnosing a cascade that is another lane's load, and worse, it can read a contention failure as a real regression. Rules: a lane running the full suite TAKES the lock and says so in its report; a lane seeing a multi-suite cascade checks for a sibling process BEFORE diagnosing its own code; and the mutex should become something the harness takes rather than something a brief asks for.
- **2026-08-22 · A LANE'S CORRECT REASONING WENT STALE UNDER IT WHILE IT WORKED.** The notify lane deep-linked ballot notices at the proposal card rather than a ballot page, reasoning correctly that "there is no ballot page in the client yet, the governance engine shipped server-side only". True at its branch point `f29deed`; false by the time it reported, because the governance UI merged at `eea810c` and `Decision.tsx`/`Decisions.tsx` now exist. **In a fast swarm, a lane's premises expire.** The coordinator owns this, not the lane: when merging, check every "X does not exist yet" claim in a report against CURRENT main, because the lane cannot see what landed after it branched.

- **2026-08-22 · A LANE WROTE A PLAUSIBLE WRONG EXPLANATION INTO CLAUDE.md, THEN CAUGHT ITSELF.** The budget lane saw a 28 KB gap between its local dist measurement and CI's, reasoned it was machine drift (Node 22 vs 25, LF vs CRLF), and committed that into the house rules. It was wrong: **the `pull_request` run builds the branch ALREADY MERGED WITH MAIN, so it carries other lanes' landed work**, while the `push` run on the same SHA matched the local machine to the kilobyte with an identical CSS content hash. It also tested and ruled out Tailwind scanning CLAUDE.md before concluding. PR #52 carries the correction. **This is the same class of error the coordinator made about the map's back button in the same hour: a plausible explanation offered without the measurement that would falsify it, and then written somewhere durable where the next reader inherits it as fact.** A wrong entry in CLAUDE.md is worse than none, because every future lane reads it as settled. Rule: an explanation that lands in the house rules carries the measurement that distinguishes it from the alternatives, or it says it is a hypothesis.

- **2026-08-22 · I DROPPED ONE OF THE FOUNDER'S ORIGINAL TEN ASKS AND HE HAD TO NOTICE IT HIMSELF.** Ask 5 from his first batch (the "back to the village" button being ugly and COVERING THE BUILD BUTTON, plus the "make the map yours" opener) was scoped as lane M1 in `ROUND5_PROPOSAL_2026-08-21.md` and never dispatched. When the round reshaped into the natural-interface waves, M1's contents were not carried across: W1c took the panel action-points, and the rest of the map chrome silently fell out of the plan. He asked "Has this not shipped yet?" and the answer was no. **The transcription was faithful and the tracking was not - a verbatim record of an ask is worthless if nothing carries it to a lane.** Rule: when a plan is REPLACED rather than extended, every ask in the old plan is reconciled item by item against the new one, and anything not carried forward is either dispatched or explicitly deferred WITH the founder told. A plan rewrite is exactly where scope goes missing, because the new plan looks complete on its own terms.
- **2026-08-22 · The build button had THREE independent gates and only one was the reported one.** Diagnosing it: (a) the shell's back button at `top:44px,left:12px` in a stacking context above the iframe covers the artifact's `#buildBtn` at `left:14px,top:52px` regardless of the artifact's z-index (his diagnosis, exactly right); (b) `grounds-v0.html:779` hides `#buildBtn` OUTRIGHT in `body.pocket`, so on a phone it is not covered, it is absent; (c) it also needs `body.can-edit` from the credentialed hand push. **A control that is invisible for three different reasons reads identically to a control that is broken for one.** Worth remembering when the next "I cannot see X" arrives: enumerate every gate before fixing the first one found.

- **2026-08-22 · TWO LANES CLAIMED MIGRATION 0090 AND BOTH FOUR-WAY SCANS WERE CORRECT.** The gov-UI lane took `0090_proposal_drafts.sql` and the honesty lane took `0090_retire_quest_propose.sql`. Each ran the full scan (remote refs, local refs, disk across all ~74 worktrees, `git worktree list`) and each was right AT THE MOMENT IT SCANNED, because the sibling had not pushed yet. **The four-way scan cannot see a concurrent lane's unpushed intention, so it is not sufficient when lanes run in parallel.** Resolution: honesty merged first and kept 0090; gov-UI renumbered to 0091 before merge, which is safe precisely because the file had never been applied anywhere (the never-renumber rule protects migrations the ledger has already recorded, and this one had not shipped). **The real fix is structural: the COORDINATOR allocates migration numbers at brief time and writes them into the brief, rather than each lane discovering one.** A scan answers "what is taken"; only the coordinator knows "what is about to be taken".

- **2026-08-22 · I TALKED RYE INTO A RULING ON A CLAIM I HAD NOT VERIFIED.** Recommending the de-iframe I told him the artifact "already has its own chrome - the top bar and the Back to the village button from your screenshots", so losing the React shell would cost him "a duplicate, not a feature". **The artifact contains "Back to the village" ZERO times** (control: "Get Involved" appears 6x). That button is the SHELL's, drawn OVER the map, which is exactly why it appeared in his screenshot. Its own header says the artifact's escapes "render off-screen on a phone", which is why the shell's button exists at all. So the change I called cheap would have left a phone visitor with no way out of the map. **Rule: a claim that a founder's decision rests on gets verified BEFORE it is offered, not after he rules on it.** The wider lesson is the same one this program keeps paying: I read a screenshot as evidence about the artifact when it was evidence about the composite of shell plus artifact.

- **2026-08-22 · I IDLED THE SWARM TO WAIT FOR A RULING THAT GATED ONE LANE.** Rye: [keep lanes going with everything else while we plan out this fix together... You should have continued going on your own until the plan was completed, not stopping like this.] I asked for the law-5 sentence and then reported and waited, while four independent lanes (governance UI, silent mutations, dead-editor wiring, the honesty sweep) sat undispatched behind a question that gated only the governance UI. The skill said "Then wait" and I read it as a stop on the round. **Fixed in the skill itself** (`~/.claude/skills/swarm-supervisor/SKILL.md` §7): a question is a dependency on ONE lane, never a stop on the round; report progress WHILE the next wave builds rather than instead of dispatching it; a round ends when the work ends, not when a good summary is written; never wind down or archive a session while lanes run or the queue is non-empty.

- **2026-08-22 · THE THIRD BROKEN-LOOP CLASS: THE INCOMPLETE LOOP.** Three classes of the same family have now each been found by a separate audit, and naming all three is what makes them huntable: (1) **no button** - an engine built, gated and e2e-tested with no client caller (cycle close, ~57 routes); (2) **no reader** - an admin write into a generic document store that no renderer selects from (27 dead editors); (3) **no feedback** - an action that changes state without telling the people who need to know. The third has three shapes: an action gives its actor no feedback (a handler that discards its result), a state change notifies nobody who can act (compare against the ~20 events that DO call notifyAdmins - absence is the finding), and a resolution never returns to whoever started it. The harassment-report defect had **all three shapes at once**, which is why it read as "the queue is missing" when the queue was only a third of it. Each class has a mechanical detector: routes without callers, writes without readers, mutations without notifications. Build all three as standing CI checks.

- **2026-08-22 · THE DEAD-EDITOR ROOT CAUSE, and it explains all 27 cases.** An admin surface is dead when its WRITE goes to a generic document store (`app_config['content']`, `journey-state`, `brand.*`, `village_brief`) instead of the table its renderer selects from; every WIRED surface writes the row the public page reads. **The two are indistinguishable in the admin UI** - same Save button, same green toast, same "Changes save to the server and go live immediately" - so a founder cannot tell which kind of editor they are using, which is why this survived. Worst: four Journey Content tabs the Setup Wizard actively points founders at, all dead; Project History's 19-page copy editor, dead; Village Brain's 14 sections never reach the guide members actually meet; and **the exit policy - the highest-stakes copy on the site - renders three blocks that have NO admin field while offering a checkbox that removes the "these are placeholders" warning, so a village can publish the platform's boilerplate as its own settled terms.** Structural fix: a save-path assertion (an admin write to a store no renderer reads is a testable defect), plus the honest-refusal convention this repo already gets right in the `stay.credit_expiry_days` handler, which REFUSES the write with an explanation rather than accepting it into a void.

- **2026-08-22 · THE MISSING-TRIGGER CLASS: engines built, tested and gated, with no button.** An adversarial sweep found ~57 server routes with zero client callers, and they are not scattered noise - they are three or four coherent features that stopped one commit short of a caller. Verified personally with a working control: `POST /api/admin/cycles/close` exists (server/index.ts:18926) and has **ZERO** callers in client/src, so **the gratitude cycle can never be settled from the product** - and with it the recognition-to-value pool distribution, badge auto-evaluation, health snapshots, and every governance proposal held for a cycle-timed dial. Meanwhile the UI counts down to it (CycleClock, "N left this cycle"). Second confirmed case: a member can Flag a DM and `GET/PUT /api/admin/messages/reports` has no client caller, so a harassment report succeeds into a void. Standing guard to build: a CI check extracting `app.<verb>("...")` from server/index.ts and failing on any route absent from every client/src file, with an explicit allowlist for webhooks, crawler surfaces and agent endpoints.
- **2026-08-22 · `git grep` SILENTLY MATCHES NOTHING when the pattern starts with `/`.** `git grep -F "/api/auth/login" origin/main` returns 0; `git grep "api/auth/login"` returns 10. An audit's first sweep declared all 492 routes uncalled on this alone. Same class as the rg-absent trap: **prove every negative against a known-present control in the same command** before believing a zero. A related trap from the same sweep: bash substring matching over a multi-megabyte command-substitution blob drops matches, turning 1 real finding into 78 false ones.

- **2026-08-22 · I dispatched a six-agent wave into a Fable 5 credit ceiling and all six died before a single tool call.** ~214K tokens bought nothing. This is verbatim the failure the skill's own §4b.3 names ("A fleet dispatched into a quota wall... Near a ceiling, spend ONE canary and read its result before committing a wave") and I did not apply it, because the session had been running on Fable 5 all day without hitting a limit so I treated headroom as proven rather than measured. Compounding error: I pinned `model: 'fable'` on all six agents explicitly, so there was no inheritance path to a model that still had credits. Rules: (1) before any wave of 3+ agents, spend ONE trivial canary on the target model and read its result; (2) a model override pinned on every agent removes the fallback that an omitted override would have given, so pin deliberately, not reflexively; (3) worktrees survived clean, which is the one thing that went right - the lanes had not started, so a re-dispatch costs only the prompt tokens.

- **2026-08-22 · A lane that yields to its own background monitors reports a mid-state as its final answer.** The #31 remediation agent started an ~18-min mutant loop, armed two monitors, and ended its turn "waiting on the monitors" — the workflow's parallel() collected that waiting note as the lane's REPORT, with the fix present but uncommitted and the dangerous old tip still on the remote. The adversarial verifier caught it (three blockers, one real defect: a non-unique mutant anchor). Rule: a workflow lane brief must say "do not end your turn while your own background jobs are the only thing holding your result — wait them out or poll them to completion inside the turn"; and a verifier that checks PUSHED state against REPORTED state earns its keep every time.

- **2026-08-21 · The wrong-repo `gh` call bit three times in one day; the third nearly merged a stranger's PR.** The shell's cwd resets to the HUB worktree between calls, and a bare `gh pr view/merge N` then answers about the hub's PR N (a closed dependabot PR shared the number and read as "20f45bc MERGEABLE" and later "Typecheck FAILURE"; a `gh pr merge 23` was attempted against it and failed only because its base had moved). Rule, mechanical: every `gh`/`git` command against game-amora starts with `cd` into the game-amora worktree IN THE SAME command; and never merge from a listing older than the current command. One wrong status was relayed to Rye (a "newer tip 20f45bc" that was the hub's dependabot branch); corrected here.

- **2026-08-21 · A guard nobody's data exercises is not a guard.** The pinned dialer was broken for every real dial on current Node, and four consumers stayed green for weeks because each short-circuited on empty data before dialing. Rule: any wrapper whose job is to TALK TO THE OUTSIDE ships with at least one test that actually dials (a local fixture host), and a job's first live tick is part of its lane's DONE.

- **2026-08-21 · The coordinator printed secrets twice while debugging a pipe.** (1) A `head -c`-truncated `railway variables` blob failed a JSON parse, and the fallback branch printed the raw first lines, exposing `ADMIN_PASSWORD` and a truncated `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. (2) The mint tool, imported under `node -e`, threw with the piped `AUTH_TOKEN_SECRET` embedded in the error text, and the coordinator's `[ $rc -ne 0 ] && echo "$TOKEN"` printed it. Both are in this machine's transcript. Rules that now bind: never print a captured pipeline's raw output on failure (error text embeds the piped input); debug secret pipelines with presence/length only; a fallback branch is subject to the same redaction as the happy path. Remedy: Rye rotates `AUTH_TOKEN_SECRET` (all sessions re-login; nothing else breaks) and optionally `ADMIN_PASSWORD`.

- **2026-08-16 · A budget priced against one gate is not priced.** The catalog art was sized against the image ratchet (bytes) and never against the dist ceiling (`du -sk` blocks): 18 × 25 KB became 508 KB on ext4 and tripped `MAX_TOTAL_DIST_KB` by 136 KB after the images were already painted. Rule: when a lane will add static bytes, price them against EVERY size gate in `ci.yml` (image ratchet, artifact budget, main JS, total dist) in the brief, in the gate's own unit.

- A worktree's name says nothing about its ref (`wt-integrate` ≠ integration home; `gov-overflow`
  sits detached at 1428603).
- A registry entry written from a handoff is a guess until confirmed from disk.
- A migration number has a FOURTH holding place: another session's scratchpad worktree.
  `git worktree list` enumerates them; scan them all.
- A green suite is a sample: read the skip count and the Duration, never the badge.
- The docs' verification SHA is not the dispatch SHA. Six commits landed between 1428603 and
  28dace2 while the program was being written; +279 lines in `server/index.ts` made every line
  anchor stale. Re-measure at dispatch, always.
- A background agent dies with the machine's sleep; a committed ledger and an on-disk worktree
  survive. Write state before starting anything long.
- **Grep the whole monorepo for callers, not `client/`** (HS PR 2): the hub's claims contributor pages are Next.js routes under `apps/gov/` reading through `fetchFromMainSite`; a `client/` grep alone would have projected them blind. Before withholding a field, enumerate callers across every app that reaches the procedure.
- **A probe that fails open reports success** (F1 resume, self-caught): reading `--tabbar-h` off the root gives the UNRESOLVED string `calc(4rem + 1px + env(...))`; `parseFloat` → NaN → `NaN || 0` → a zero-height band → every control "clear", including the one the lane was sent to fix. Measure the rendered element, and make a probe's failure loud (a NaN band is a bug, not a pass). Also: WebKit-on-Windows reads safe-area insets as 0, so any measured band is the OPTIMISTIC edge — clearances must hold with room.
- **Measure with the modules ON** (F3, 2026-08-15, self-caught on its own closing proof): the store's 360px overflow is data-gated — the lifecycle hint that carries the long token renders only for a non-core module that is not off; a scratch seed with everything off cannot show it, so F3's "0 overflow" at its HEAD was true of the wrong state. Same lesson as `page.route`, one layer up: prove the control APPLIED (the hint line is on screen) before believing a zero; give scratch schemas live's lifecycle shape. Also: any listing field can be one long token — harden the shared column, not the one paragraph.
- **`elementFromPoint` ownership must be strict** (V2 harness, self-caught): `hit.contains(el)` accepts an ancestor, so `<body>` "owns" every probe and every hit area measures the whole viewport. Use `hit === el || el.contains(hit)`.
- **`scrollIntoView` is async under `scroll-behavior: smooth`** (V2, self-caught): a rect read right after is stale and every probe point clips — 36/46 landing controls were falsely flagged unreachable (true: 0). Force `scroll-behavior:auto` in the harness; never flag a control that got zero probe points. Also `locator.click()` times out on `/map` (animating canvas defeats actionability) — use raw `mouse.click`.
- **A timer must not charge a click's budget** (K2, self-caught): `assistantDailyCapReached` calls `overLimit`, which INSERTS a `rate_hits` row — right for a request, wrong for a poll running 288×/day against a 600 cap; the job would have eaten half the village's assistant budget by ticking. Gate on a read; charge per submitted request.
- **The scratchpad is not lane-isolated** (K2): a generic `baseline.txt` there belonged to another lane and was nearly clobbered — every lane writes under its own subdirectory.
- **A contribution check greps ADDED lines, not changed files** (validate-module.mjs, caught on P's PR #5 after the classifier fix exposed it): the six security greps ran over every file the diff touched, so P's 335 clean added lines in `server/index.ts` were blamed for 41 pre-existing raw queries elsewhere in the same 18k-line file — every server PR would block at stage 6. Scope tracked-file greps to `git diff -U0` `^+` lines; whole-file only for NEW files; surface pre-existing debt as informational. Same shape as F1's lesson: measure what THIS change did, not what the file contains.
- **A hub `.env` may point at production, and a BOM can hide it from a key-grep** (Lane P): the hub worktree's `.env` carried `DATABASE_URL` → the production proxy behind a UTF-8 BOM; a `^[A-Za-z_]` grep missed it. Vitest does not load `.env`, which is the only reason the DB suites skipped instead of running against production. Strip the BOM in the grep, and never let a hub lane run DB suites without first checking which host `DATABASE_URL` names.
- **`$?` after a pipe is the pipe's last command** (Lane P, 2026-08-15, self-caught; the same trap as `tsc | head` in the failure catalogue): P's first gate script read `$?` after `cmd | tail`, reporting `tail`'s exit for every gate. Capture the exit before the pipe (`set -o pipefail` or `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`), and treat any gate table produced by a script that pipes as suspect until that is shown.
- **A narrowed question is not a lookup** (K1, 2026-08-15): "what did we decide" and "what did
  we decide about quiet hours" scored identically in the router; a template listing every
  decision would have answered the second confidently, for free, and wrongly — and it was live
  in two e2e fixtures. Deterministic answers must detect scope narrowing and hand narrowed
  questions to the model with the data prefetched. Corollary: an empty shelf honestly answers
  "what did we decide about X" (nothing recorded) but must NOT answer an advisory question
  ("how should we structure our circles") — that is not a record lookup at all.
- **The gate set grows under you — enumerate `ci.yml`, never trust the brief's count** (F4,
  2026-08-15, applied correctly): its brief said eleven; Lane D's merge had just added a twelfth
  (doc-link guard). F4 listed every `run:` step in the file it was about to be judged by and ran
  the new one. The ledger's §5 now says so in its own header, and Lane I's image ratchet will
  make it thirteen.
- **The seed fix and the volume copy are both needed** (F4, clarifying the record): Lane I's
  copy fixes LIVE (rows already carry the paths); the seed at `imageUrl: null` is what keeps a
  FRESH fork from inheriting 14 dead paths (its volume is empty). Different halves of one
  finding; neither replaces the other.
- **A gate must classify on markers, never on prose — and it must live where it can be tested**
  (Lane D's intake workflow, caught at merge of PR #6, fixed in #7): the blocking-stage
  classifier grepped the bare string `clause 14`; an informational validator line contained it,
  and the workflow failed its own PR. D's deeper diagnosis: the classifier lived in YAML, so it
  only ran when a PR ran it — it shipped a proof that violations block and NO proof that a clean
  tree passes, and the same author wrote both the gate and its measurement. Rule: decide on
  structured output; extract gate logic to a script with a test whose FIRST case is the clean
  path; prove both directions.
- **Release only the lock you acquired** (Lane F4, self-caught): a mutex helper that runs
  `rmdir .test-lock` unconditionally after a timed-out poll tries to delete another lane's
  lock; F4's survived only because K2 writes a marker file inside the directory. Track
  acquisition; release only then; write your own marker.
- **`!img.alt` is not "missing alt"** (Lane V's detector, caught by F1): a truthiness test
  cannot tell "attribute absent" from "attribute present and empty", and `alt=""` is the
  REQUIRED markup for a decorative image — the check inverts the standard it enforces. Test
  `hasAttribute('alt')`. Two detectors disagreed (L: 0 missing; V: 6); the DOM settled it.
- **A brief's root-cause hypothesis is a hypothesis** (F1, 2026-08-15): the coordinator's
  brief said "the spacer is missing or bypassed"; F1 measured that it applies on every route
  and could never fix first paint, found the real cause (a fixed bar owning viewport pixels at
  every offset), and proved the literal target (19→0) unreachable without recreating the
  original defect. Write the target as the HARM metric (named CTAs own their centre at first
  paint; zero controls unreachable), not as a count that a sampled sweep happens to produce.
- **A control that cannot fail loudly is not a control** (Lane F3, 2026-08-15): under
  Playwright WebKit, `page.route` interception fired zero handlers while the request still
  went out, so the "control payload" run reported a clean result it had never applied;
  Chromium was unaffected, which made it plausible. Fix: patch the page's own `fetch` and hard-
  assert the control reached the DOM (`control landed: true`). Any injected control needs a
  positive assertion that it was injected.
- **`||` and `??` are not interchangeable in a sweep** (Lane F3): the `d.error || "..."` sites
  used a falsy fallback; a mechanical `d.message ?? d.error` returns `""` for an empty-string
  body — a blank toast where the call site's own words used to be. Read the operator you are
  replacing, not just the field.
- **A contract change's sweep covers every READER of the field, not the reported sites**
  (Lane F4, 2026-08-15): the coordinator routed the 57 `d.error` renderers; F4 also swept
  every client read of `.message` on a response body and found `OnchainCard.tsx` using
  `!ch.message` as a proxy for "challenge failed" — after 401s gained a `message`, a
  signed-out member's wallet would have been asked to sign the string "Sign in first". The
  reported list is a floor. When you change what a field means, grep for every consumer of
  every field you touched, including the ones you added.
- **A simultaneous stall across every lane is infrastructure, and the worktrees survive it**
  (coordinator, 2026-08-15): all four fix lanes hit "no progress for 600s" within the same
  minute — machine sleep / connection loss, not four code failures. Recovery that worked:
  inspect every worktree BEFORE resuming anything (HEAD, ahead-of-main, remote ref, dirty
  files); every lane had real uncommitted work in its zone and nothing pushed; resume each
  from its own transcript with "commit your work first with `git add -p`, then continue."
  Never re-dispatch fresh over a worktree with dirty files — that is how work gets done twice
  or reverted. Also brief every long lane to COMMIT (not push) at each milestone so a drop
  costs minutes, not the lane.
- **`networkidle` never fires on this app** (Lane V, 2026-08-15): `/api/game/pulse` and the
  notification poller keep the connection busy, so any Playwright navigation awaiting
  `networkidle` burns its full timeout on every route (Lane L saw the same on `/map`). Use
  `domcontentloaded` + a fixed settle (~3.5s), write results incrementally per viewport so a
  stall can never cost a whole run, and never read a missing results file as a clean pass.
  Put this in every future live-QA brief.
- **Verify the recipient before messaging a lane** (coordinator, 2026-08-15, self-caught via
  Lane Q's report): two mid-flight guidance messages (mobile-first design input; the research
  relay) were sent to Lane Q's handle believing it was Lane M — two same-block dispatches
  return handles in call order and I read them swapped. Cost: Lane M worked without its
  design input for one phase; Lane Q correctly ignored both and reported the misroute. Rule:
  before any SendMessage to a lane, confirm the handle against the dispatch result's task
  description, not against memory. (Both messages re-sent to the true Lane M in full.)
- **A search's silence is not a negative** (Lane S, 2026-08-14, self-caught): `rg` missing from
  PATH died at exit 127 and its empty output read as "no matches"; a `find` that scanned zero
  files returned exit 0; an indexed Grep returned 1 file when a bounded search proved at least 7
  contain the string. Rule: before trusting any negative, prove the same pipeline returns matches
  on a known-present pattern, and prefer shape-based searches (the credential's format) over
  name-based ones (the variable you guessed).
- **Relay research verbatim with its source, never compressed into a claim** (coordinator,
  2026-08-15): my relay to Lane M merged Atlassian's knowledge-base policy figure (90 days) and
  a contract clause (45-day takedown) into one sentence; M correctly refused to transcribe it.
  A lane refusing a coordinator relay is a control working; ratify the refusal.
- **Hazards are per-repo** (coordinator, 2026-08-15): I briefed Lane P (hub) with the
  game-amora memory "CI has two gates CLAUDE.md omits" (bundle budget + audit); the hub has no
  bundle budget and its audit is advisory. Any remembered fact about "the CI" names which CI.
- **The gate count in a brief is stale the moment a lane merges** (coordinator, 2026-08-15):
  F4's brief said eleven while D's merge had made it twelve; F4 caught it by enumerating
  `ci.yml`. §5's header now says enumerate; briefs say "this list may have grown".
- **Two coordinators now share `wt/integration`** (2026-08-16): a round-4 session committed
  five times to this branch while the round-3 session was still open. Rule for both: pull
  before every write, edit the ledger and handoff by hunk (never wholesale `Write` from an
  in-memory copy), stage by path, and leave the other session's files alone. The
  swarm-supervisor skill (v1.1.0, 2026-08-16) and `PROMPT_NEXT_COORDINATOR.md` carry every
  lesson in this section in generic form.

The fix stops new reads; tokens already taken remain valid until rotated.
